| [Ch 410](chapters/410.md) | **Autoimmune Sclerosing Cholangitis (ASC)** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Overlap of AIH + bile duct sclerosis on MRCP in 20–30% of pediatric cases; strongly linked to ulcerative colitis; add ursodeoxycholic acid (UDCA) to immunosuppression |
| [Ch 410](chapters/410.md) | **Standard Immunosuppressive Regimen** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Prednisone 1–2 mg/kg/day (max 60 mg) + Azathioprine 1–2 mg/kg/day (verify normal TPMT/NUDT15 first); liver biopsy showing interface plasma-cell hepatitis required |
| [Ch 411](chapters/411.md) | **Acetaminophen (Intrinsic Zone 3 Necrosis)** | `CYP2E1` | The Digestive System | `—` | CYP2E1 → NAPQI depletes glutathione; AST/ALT >3,000 U/L; Rumack-Matthew nomogram at ≥4 hr; antidote: N-acetylcysteine (NAC) |
| [Ch 411](chapters/411.md) | **Valproic Acid (Mitochondrial Microvesicular Steatosis)** | `POLG, CPS1` | mtDNA | `—` | Inhibits mitochondrial β-oxidation & CPS1; highest risk <2 yr or with POLG variants; antidote: IV L-carnitine (100 mg/kg/day) |
| [Ch 411](chapters/411.md) | **Lamotrigine / Carbamazepine / Phenytoin (DRESS)** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Aromatic anticonvulsant hypersensitivity 2–8 wk after start: fever, eosinophilia, facial edema, hepatitis; stop drug + systemic corticosteroids |
| [Ch 411](chapters/411.md) | **Minocycline (Autoimmune-Like DILI)** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Adolescents treated for acne developing arthralgias, fever, ANA+, hypergammaglobulinemia, interface hepatitis; resolves after stopping minocycline ± steroids |
| [Ch 412](chapters/412.md) | **PALF Diagnostic Criteria (Vitamin K–Refractory)** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | INR ≥1.5 (PT ≥15 s) WITH hepatic encephalopathy, OR INR ≥2.0 (PT ≥20 s) WITHOUT encephalopathy (after IV Vitamin K administration) |
| [Ch 412](chapters/412.md) | **Neonatal PALF (<28 Days) Must-Not-Miss** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | HSV (empiric IV acyclovir 20 mg/kg q8h!), GALD (IVIG + exchange transfusion), Galactosemia (soy formula), Tyrosinemia type 1 (urine succinylacetone → nitisinone) |
| [Ch 412](chapters/412.md) | **Older Child PALF Must-Not-Miss** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Acetaminophen (NAC), Fulminant Wilson Disease (Coombs-neg hemolysis, Alk Phos:Bili 2.2 → liver transplant), Autoimmune Hepatitis (anti-LKM-1/ANA → steroids) |
| [Ch 412](chapters/412.md) | **PICU Neuroprotection in PALF** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Continuous D10W–D20W (glucose 90–140 mg/dL), HOB 30°, 3% hypertonic saline (Na 145–155 mEq/L), CRRT for ammonia >200 µmol/L; do NOT mask INR with routine FFP |
| [Ch 413](chapters/413.md) | **Todani Type I Choledochal Cyst (80–90%)** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Fusiform/cystic dilatation of extrahepatic CBD; associated with anomalous pancreaticobiliary junction; treat with complete cyst excision + Roux-en-Y hepaticojejunostomy |
| [Ch 413](chapters/413.md) | **Caroli Disease (Todani Type V) vs Caroli Syndrome** | `PKHD1` | The Digestive System | `—` | Caroli disease: isolated saccular intrahepatic duct dilatation ('central dot sign'); Caroli syndrome: intrahepatic ectasia + congenital hepatic fibrosis + ARPKD (PKHD1) |
| [Ch 413](chapters/413.md) | **Congenital Hepatic Fibrosis (CHF)** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Ductal plate malformation causing presinusoidal portal hypertension (splenomegaly, esophageal varices) with normal hepatocellular synthetic function |
| [Ch 413](chapters/413.md) | **Malignancy Risk in Choledochal Cysts** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | High lifetime risk of cholangiocarcinoma if cyst wall is left in situ; internal drainage without excision is contraindicated |
| [Ch 414](chapters/414.md) | **Black Pigment Gallstones** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Calcium bilirubinate; chronic hemolysis (sickle cell disease, hereditary spherocytosis, thalassemia), TPN, ileal resection; radiopaque in ~50% |
| [Ch 414](chapters/414.md) | **Ceftriaxone Biliary Pseudolithiasis** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Reversible calcium-ceftriaxone precipitates in gallbladder; resolves spontaneously within weeks of stopping ceftriaxone; surgery NOT indicated |
| [Ch 414](chapters/414.md) | **Gallbladder Hydrops in Pediatrics** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Acute acalculous non-inflammatory distension of gallbladder; classic association with Kawasaki disease and group A streptococcal infection; self-resolving with medical therapy |
| [Ch 414](chapters/414.md) | **Symptomatic Cholelithiasis / Choledocholithiasis** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Laparoscopic cholecystectomy for symptomatic stones or hemolytic disease; ERCP stone extraction for common bile duct obstruction |
| [Ch 415](chapters/415.md) | **Extrahepatic Portal Vein Obstruction (EHPVO)** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Prior neonatal umbilical vein catheterization/omphalitis; splenomegaly + variceal bleed with NORMAL liver synthetic function; 'cavernous transformation' on Doppler US; Meso-Rex bypass |
| [Ch 415](chapters/415.md) | **Intrahepatic Sinusoidal Portal Hypertension** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Biliary atresia (#1), AIH, Wilson disease, CFLD; splenomegaly + varices + impaired hepatic synthetic function/cirrhosis; definitive cure via liver transplantation |
| [Ch 415](chapters/415.md) | **Acute Variceal Hemorrhage Protocol** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Restrictive RBC transfusion (Hb target 7–8 g/dL) + IV Octreotide (1–2 µg/kg bolus, 1–5 µg/kg/hr) + IV Ceftriaxone + Endoscopic Variceal Ligation (EVL) |
| [Ch 415](chapters/415.md) | **Meso-Rex Bypass vs Distal Splenorenal Shunt** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Meso-Rex shunt (SMV to intrahepatic left portal vein) restores physiological portal flow in EHPVO and reverses hepatopulmonary/neurocognitive sequelae |
| [Ch 416](chapters/416.md) | **Top Pediatric Liver Transplant Indications** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | 1. Biliary atresia (35–45%), 2. Metabolic/genetic diseases (20%), 3. Pediatric acute liver failure (15%), 4. Unresectable hepatoblastoma (8–10%) |
| [Ch 416](chapters/416.md) | **PELD Score (<12 Years Old)** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Incorporates Total Bilirubin, INR, Serum Albumin, Age <1 yr, and Growth Failure (weight/height z-score < −2 SD); MELD used ≥12 yr |
| [Ch 416](chapters/416.md) | **Hepatic Artery Thrombosis (HAT)** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Most common & severe early vascular complication (<14 days) in small pediatric grafts; causes biliary necrosis/graft loss; screen with daily Doppler US |
| [Ch 416](chapters/416.md) | **EBV-Associated PTLD** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | B-cell lymphoproliferation in EBV-naive pediatric recipients; monitor serial blood EBV PCR; first-line therapy: reduce immunosuppression + Rituximab (anti-CD20) |
| [Ch 417](chapters/417.md) | **Mesenteric & Omental Lymphatic Cysts** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Thin-walled multilocular chylous/serous cysts in ileal mesentery or omentum; present as mobile mass or acute volvulus; surgical excision |
| [Ch 417](chapters/417.md) | **Enteric Duplication Cyst** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Mesenteric border of ileum (#1); shares muscular wall & blood supply with bowel; ultrasound 'double-layered gut signature'; resect with adjacent bowel |
| [Ch 417](chapters/417.md) | **Congenital Transmesenteric Internal Hernia** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Herniation of small bowel through a defect in the small-bowel mesentery without a hernia sac; high risk of rapid strangulation and gangrene |
| [Ch 417](chapters/417.md) | **Persistent Vitelline (Omphalomesenteric) Band** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Fibrous band from distal ileum to umbilicus acting as a pivot point for closed-loop small bowel obstruction and volvulus |
| [Ch 418](chapters/418.md) | **SAAG ≥ 1.1 g/dL (Portal Hypertension)** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Cirrhosis, biliary atresia, Budd-Chiari syndrome, post-Fontan / right heart failure; treat with Na restriction + Spironolactone ± Furosemide |
| [Ch 418](chapters/418.md) | **SAAG < 1.1 g/dL (Non-Portal Hypertension)** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Pancreatic ascites (high ascitic amylase), tuberculous peritonitis, peritoneal malignancy, lupus serositis |
| [Ch 418](chapters/418.md) | **Chylous Ascites (Subchapter 418.1)** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Milky fluid, triglycerides >200 mg/dL, lymphocyte predominance; congenital lymphangiectasia (#1 in infants) or surgical/abusive trauma; treat with MCT-based diet ± Octreotide/TPN |
| [Ch 418](chapters/418.md) | **Tense Ascites Paracentesis Protocol** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Therapeutic paracentesis with concurrent IV 25% Albumin infusion (0.5–1 g/kg) to prevent intravascular volume collapse and hepatorenal syndrome |
| [Ch 419](chapters/419.md) | **Acute Primary Peritonitis / SBP (Subchapter 419.1)** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Nephrotic syndrome or cirrhosis; monomicrobial (S. pneumoniae, E. coli, Klebsiella); ascitic PMN ≥250 cells/mm³; treat with IV Ceftriaxone/Cefotaxime (medical, non-surgical) |
| [Ch 419](chapters/419.md) | **Acute Secondary Peritonitis (Subchapter 419.2)** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Perforated appendicitis (#1 in children) or neonatal NEC; polymicrobial (E. coli + Bacteroides fragilis); free air on imaging; urgent surgical source control + Pip-Tazo or Ceftriaxone/Metronidazole |
| [Ch 419](chapters/419.md) | **Localized Peritoneal Abscess (Subchapter 419.3)** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Pelvic, interloop, or subphrenic abscess post-appendicitis; spiking fevers & tenesmus; US/CT-guided percutaneous drainage + IV antibiotics |
| [Ch 419](chapters/419.md) | **CAPD (Peritoneal Dialysis) Peritonitis** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Cloudy dialysate effluent with WBC >100/mm³ (>50% PMNs); Staphylococcus epidermidis or S. aureus (#1) or Pseudomonas; intraperitoneal vancomycin + ceftazidime/cefepime |
| [Ch 420](chapters/420.md) | **Epigastric Hernia** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Small defect in linea alba between xiphoid and umbilicus; contains preperitoneal fat; pea-sized midline mass; does NOT resolve spontaneously → elective surgical closure |
| [Ch 420](chapters/420.md) | **Diastasis Recti (Differential Diagnosis)** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Benign thinning/widening of linea alba without a discrete fascial defect; bulges only when crying/straining; 0% incarceration risk; reassurance only (no surgery) |
| [Ch 420](chapters/420.md) | **Umbilical Hernia (Differential Diagnosis)** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Patent umbilical ring; >85–90% close spontaneously by age 4–5 years; observe unless defect >1.5–2 cm persisting past age 4–5 yr or incarcerated |
| [Ch 420](chapters/420.md) | **Incisional Hernia (Subchapter 420.1)** | `—` | The Digestive System | `—` | Fascial dehiscence at prior laparotomy scar (risk factors: wound infection, steroids, ascites); high incarceration risk in small defects → surgical repair |
